The convergence study used T = 0.05, r = 0.2 and N = 16, 32, 64, 128 and 256. Doubling N reduced both error norms by approximately four, with observed orders close to 2 (2.0058 to 2.0001 for L2,h). This matches the expected O(Δt + h²) accuracy, since Δt = rh². The complete table and log-lo
Appendix D. Serial convergence study
For consecutive refinements, p = log₂(E(N) / E(2N)). The observed order is approximately 2, as predicted for the stable scheme with fixed r and Δt proportional to h².
Appendix E. Deliberate instability test
The error is still small at 300 steps, becomes visible around 400 steps, and grows rapidly by 500 steps. We reproduced the saved intermediate fields from the experimental source to inspect where the error grows; the largest errors are in the plate interior, near the center. These snapshots
Figure E1. Numerical error relative to the exact solution at 400 and 500 steps. Each image has its own scale.
Appendix E (continued). Final unstable field
Figure E2. Measured temperature field at 1,000 steps. The values reach approximately ±6 × 10¹⁶ and alternate in a checkerboard pattern.

6      Part 2: 1D strip decomposition

6.1     Decomposition

Split the N − 1 interior rows among the P processes in contiguous strips, as evenly as possible:
when N − 1 is not divisible by P , the first (N − 1) mod P ranks get one extra row. Rank 0 owns
the bottom strip and rank P − 1 the top one.

## main...origin/main
# Part 1 — Serial heat solver (Step 2 working version)

This folder contains the active serial solver from the team project, with a minimal interface/output update. The numerical stencil, initialization, boundary cases, time-step selection, error calculations, and binary output are unchanged from the supplied `src/serial.cpp`.

## Build (Ubuntu / Linux)

```bash
make
```

This builds `./heat` using C++17, `-O2`, and `-ffp-contract=off`.

## Run

```bash
./heat --decomp serial --case V --N 16 --r 0.2 --T 0.05 --out verification.bin
./heat --decomp serial --case 1 --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 10
./heat --decomp serial --case 2 --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 1 --debug
```

Supported options: `--decomp serial`, `--case 1|2|V`, `--N`, `--r`, `--T` *or* `--steps`, `--out`, `--debug`, `--help`.

Only a **single `key=value` summary line** goes to standard output on successful runs. `--debug` prints the full grid to standard error. `t_total` is the step-loop duration in this serial program and equals `t_comp`; `t_comm=0`. Setup and binary output are excluded from the timing.

`--out` writes the `(N+1) x (N+1)` field as native binary doubles (little-endian on the course's x86-64 Ubuntu VMs), with `i` varying fastest.

## Step 2 change scope

- Accept the `--decomp serial` argument required by the common interface.
- Reject other decomposition modes in this **serial-only** executable.
- Build `./heat` in one command rather than `src/serial`.
- Print only one machine-readable summary on stdout, including `t_total`.
- Make full-grid printing opt-in via `--debug` (stderr).

The final project-wide `heat` integration with MPI strips and blocks is a later step. Convergence, instability scripts and retained results from the teammate's original archive will be brought into the final Part 1 directory in later steps.

+| Option | Meaning |
+| --- | --- |
+| \`--decomp serial\` | Select the serial solver. This is the default. |
+| \`--case 1\\|2\\|V\` | Four hot edges, hot horizontal edges with cold side interiors, or the exact-solution verification case. Case 2 corners are hot. |
+| \`--N\` | Number of intervals per axis. Grid spacing is \`h = 1/N\`. |
+| \`--r\` | Requested upper bound on \`dt/h²\`, with \`0 < r <= 0.25\`. |
+| \`--T\` | Final simulated time. The solver chooses enough equal steps to reach it without exceeding the requested \`r\`. |
+| \`--steps\` | Fixed step count at the requested \`r\`. Use either this or \`--T\`. |
+| \`--out\` | Write the final grid to a raw binary file. |

from pathlib import Path
from zipfile import ZipFile
from io import BytesIO
import csv
import math
import re
from PIL import Image
from docx import Document
from docx.enum.table import WD_TABLE_ALIGNMENT, WD_CELL_VERTICAL_ALIGNMENT
from docx.enum.text import WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH
from docx.oxml import OxmlElement
from docx.oxml.ns import qn
from docx.shared import Inches, Pt, RGBColor

repo = Path.cwd()
results = repo / 'part1' / 'results'
output = repo / 'output' / 'part1'
output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
reference = Path('C:/Users/User/Desktop/Part1_Report_Section_and_Appendices.docx')
with ZipFile(reference) as package:
    convergence_plot = package.read('word/media/image1.png')
    intermediate_plot = package.read('word/media/image2.png')
    final_plot = package.read('word/media/image3.png')

with (results / 'convergence.csv').open(newline='', encoding='utf-8') as source:
    convergence = list(csv.DictReader(source))

instability = []
for steps in (100, 300, 400, 500, 600, 1000):
    log = (results / f'instability_{steps}.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
    max_error = float(re.search(r'^Max error = (\S+)', log, re.M).group(1))
    instability.append((steps, max_error))
rendered_errors = ('0.00185452', '0.00009548', '0.00060731', '1.303888', '2,822.024', '6.192109 × 10¹⁶')
assert all(round(e, 8) == e for _, e in instability[:3])

def node(parent, tag, **attrs):
    elm = OxmlElement('w:' + tag)
    for attr, value in attrs.items():
        elm.set(qn('w:' + attr), str(value))
    parent.append(elm)
    return elm

def new_document(title):
    doc = Document()
    sec = doc.sections[0]
    sec.page_width, sec.page_height = Inches(8.27), Inches(11.69)
    sec.left_margin, sec.right_margin = Inches(.73), Inches(.73)
    sec.top_margin, sec.bottom_margin = Inches(.66), Inches(.66)
    styles = doc.styles
    for name, font, size, bold in [
        ('Normal', 'Aptos', 10.5, False),
        ('Title', 'Aptos', 16,<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="4195" />Task 7.1 Expected convergence order', page=True)
body(numerical, 'Task 5.5 gives error terms of order Δt and h². Keeping r fixed makes Δt = rh²/α, so both terms scale as h². For the stable, smooth verification problem we expect second-order convergence in h. Halving h should reduce the error by about a factor of four.')

heading(numerical, 'Task 7.2 Convergence study')
body(numerical, '(a) The serial solver ran Case V to T = 0.05 for N = 16, 32, 64, 128, and 256 with r = 0.2. At each of these grid sizes, T/(rh²) is an integer and r is below the stability limit.')
body(numerical, '(b) At T we compared each grid with the exact field sin(πx) sin(πy) exp(−2π²T). We report the maximum absolute nodal error and the discrete L₂ error, h times the square root of the sum of squared nodal errors.')
convergence_table(numerical, 'Measured Case V errors and observed L₂ orders.')
figure(numerical, convergence_plot, 6.20, 'Log-log plot of the two measured errors against grid spacing h, with a second-order reference line.',
       'The measured maximum and discrete L2 errors decrease in parallel with an order-two trend.')
body(numerical, '(c) Using p = log₂[E(h)/E(h/2)] for the discrete L₂ error, the orders approach 2 from 2.0058 to 2.0001. The measured behavior agrees with the prediction in Task 7.1.')
body(numerical, 'Sources. Numerical Methods for the Heat Equation, Tasks 3.2, 3.4, 5.2, 5.5, 6.3, 6.7, 7.1, and 7.2. The measured values and plots come from the Part 1 solver logs and verification fields.')
numerical.save(output / 'Part1_Numerical_Solutions.docx')
print('\n'.join(str(p) for p in sorted(output.glob('*.docx'))))
`;

import hashlib
import os
from pathlib import Path
from docx import Document
from docx.shared import Pt

source = Path(os.environ['PART1_WORD_REPORT'])
out = Path(os.environ['PART1_REPORT_QA'])
print('Original SHA256', hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
doc = Document(source)
paragraphs = doc.paragraphs
start = next(i for i, p in enumerate(paragraphs) if p.text == 'Appendix F Reproducing the serial tests')
for p in paragraphs[start + 1:]:
    p._element.getparent().remove(p._element)

def heading(text):
    doc.add_heading(text, 2)

def body(text):
    doc.add_paragraph(text)

def commands(text):
    p = doc.add_paragraph()
    p.paragraph_format.keep_together = True
    p.paragraph_format.line_spacing = 1.00
    p.paragraph_format.space_after = Pt(6)
    run = p.add_run(text)
    run.font.name = 'Consolas'
    run.font.size = Pt(9)
    with (out / 'commands.sh').open('a', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n') as script:
        script.write(text + '\n\n')

body('Start in the part1 directory on Ubuntu, using Bash. The plotting scripts need Python 3 and Matplotlib. Each run saves its results in a new temporary folder.')
heading('Build both programs')
commands('make heat unstable_demo\n'
         'part1="$PWD"\n'
         'run_dir=$(mktemp -d)\n'
         'cd "$run_dir"\n'
         'mkdir results')
heading('Case V convergence')
commands('for N in 16 32 64 128 256; do\n'
         '    "$part1/heat" --case V --N "$N" --r 0.2 --T 0.05\n'
         'done > results/convergence_runs.txt\n'
         'python3 "$part1/scripts/plot_convergence.py"')
body('The script reads the new log and creates convergence.csv and a log-log plot of the discrete L₂ error. The observed order should approach 2.')
heading('Fixed boundaries and stability check')
commands('for c in 1 2; do\n'
         '    "$part1/heat" --case "$c" --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 20 --debug\n'
         'done\n'
         '"$part1/heat" --case V --N 16 --r 0.2625 --steps 1000')
b<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="109" />field" > "$log"\n'
         'done\n'
         'python3 "$part1/scripts/plot_instability.py"\n'
         'printf \'Results are in %s/results\\n\' "$run_dir"\n'
         'cd "$part1"')
body('The logs give the errors in Table E1. The plotting script creates the 1,000-step checkerboard. Figure E1 uses the 400- and 500-step fields with the exact Case V solution subtracted at their respective final times.')

doc.save(out / source.name)
print('Corrected report', out / source.name)
`;

import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
from docx import Document
from docx.shared import Pt

source = Path(os.environ['PART1_WORD_REPORT'])
out = Path(os.environ['PART1_REPORT_QA'])
print('Original SHA256', hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
doc = Document(source)
paragraphs = doc.paragraphs
start = next(i for i, p in enumerate(paragraphs) if p.text == 'Appendix F Reproducing the serial tests')
for p in paragraphs[start + 1:]:
    p._element.getparent().remove(p._element)

doc.add_paragraph('Start in the part1 directory on Ubuntu, using Bash. The plotting scripts need Python 3 and Matplotlib. Each run saves its results in a new temporary folder.')
groups = json.loads(r'''${JSON.stringify(groups)}''')
for title, block, explanation in groups:
    doc.add_heading(title, 2)
    p = doc.add_paragraph()
    p.paragraph_format.keep_together = True
    p.paragraph_format.line_spacing = 1.00
    p.paragraph_format.space_after = Pt(6)
    run = p.add_run(block)
    run.font.name = 'Consolas'
    run.font.size = Pt(9)
    if explanation:
        doc.add_paragraph(explanation)

(out / 'commands.sh').write_text('\n\n'.join(row[1] for row in groups) + '\n', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n')
doc.save(out / source.name)
print('Corrected report', out / source.name)
`;

import os
from pathlib import Path
from docx import Document
from docx.oxml.ns import qn
from lxml import etree

paths = Path(os.environ['PART1_REPORT_QA']), Path(os.environ['PART1_WORD_REPORT'])
old = Document(paths[1])
revised = Document(paths[0] / paths[1].name)
revised.add_paragraph(old.paragraphs[-1].text)

def before_appendix(doc):
    chunks = []
    for child in doc._element.body:
        chunks.append(etree.tostring(child, method='c14n', exclusive=True))
        txt = ''.join(t.text or '' for t in child.iter(qn('w:t')))
        if txt == 'Appendix F Reproducing the serial tests':
            return chunks
    raise ValueError('Appendix F heading missing')

assert before_appendix(old) == before_appendix(revised)
revised.save(paths[0] / paths[1].name)
print('Main section and earlier appendices verified unchanged')
`;

user: # Appendix F Reproducing the serial tests

user: Start in the part1 directory on Ubuntu, using Bash. The plotting scripts need Python 3 and Matplotlib. Each run saves its results in a new temporary folder.

user: ## Build both programs

user: make heat unstable_demo\
user: &#x20;part1="$PWD"\
user: &#x20;run_dir=$(mktemp -d)\
user: &#x20;cd "$run_dir"\
user: &#x20;mkdir results

user: ## Case V convergence

user: for N in 16 32 64 128 256; do\
user: &#x20;    "$part1/heat" --case V --N "$N" --r 0.2 --T 0.05\
user: &#x20;done > results/convergence_runs.txt\
user: &#x20;python3 "$part1/scripts/plot_convergence.py"



user: The script reads the new log and creates convergence.csv and a log-log plot of the discrete L₂ error. The observed order should approach 2.

user: ## Fixed boundaries and stability check

user: for c in 1 2; do\
user: &#x20;    "$part1/heat" --case "$c" --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 20 --debug\
user: &#x20;done\
user: &#x20;"$part1/heat" --case V --N 16 --r 0.2625 --steps 1000



user: The printed grids show the fixed boundaries and temperatures in [0, 1]. The final command is expected to fail because r = 0.2625 exceeds the stable limit.

user: ## Deliberate instability

user: demo_args=(--case V --N 16 --r 0.2625)\
user: &#x20;for n in 100 300 400 500 600 1000; do\
user: &#x20;    field="results/instability\_$n.bin"\
user: &#x20;    log="results/instability\_$n.txt"\
user: &#x20;    "$part1/unstable_demo" "${demo_args[@]}" --steps "$n" --out "$field" > "$log"\
user: &#x20;done\
user: &#x20;python3 "$part1/scripts/plot_instability.py"\
user: &#x20;printf 'Results are in %s/results\n' "$run_dir"\
user: &#x20;cd "$part1"



user: The logs give the errors in Table E1. The plotting script creates the 1,000-step checkerboard. Figure E1 uses the 400- and 500-step fields with the exact Case V solution subtracted at their respective final times.



user: The tables and figures use the Part 1 solver logs and verification fields. The test definitions are given in the project specification, Section 5, and in Numerical Methods for the Heat Equation, Tasks 6.7 and 7.2. i need to execute it?

1.4       What the Laplacian means
The Laplacian has a geometric interpretation that is worth carrying with you into the rest of the
course. For a smooth function u in two dimensions, the average of u over a small circle of radius
r centred at (x, y) satisfies
                    Z 2π
                1                                                       r2 2
                           u(x + r cos ϕ, y + r sin ϕ) dϕ = u(x, y) +     ∇ u(x, y) + O(r4 ).     (1.2)
               2π    0                                                  4

So ∇2 u(x, y) is, up to a positive factor, how much the average of the surroundings exceeds the
value at the point. Read the heat equation with this in mind: a point heats up (ut > 0) when its
surroundings are on average warmer than it, and cools down when they are colder.

import math,importlib.util
for i in range(1,11):
 h=10**-i
 print('roundoff',i,abs((math.sin(1-h)-2*math.sin(1)+math.sin(1+h))/h**2+math.sin(1)))
exact=math.exp(-50)
prev=None
for n in [8000,16000,32000,64000,128000]:
 y=1.
 for j in range(n): y*=1-50/n
 E=abs(y-exact)
 print('FE',n,1/n,E,(math.log2(prev/E) if prev else None))
 prev=E
for n in [20,24,25,26,50]:
 a,b=1.,1.
 for j in range(n):
  a*=1-50/n
  b/=1+50/n
 print('stability',n,1/n,1-50/n,abs(a),b)
for n in [1000,4000]:
 k=math.ceil(.4*n*n)
 print('cost',n,k,6*(n-1)**2*k/1e9)
print('matplotlib',importlib.util.find_spec('matplotlib'))
`;

for p in range(1, 11):
    h = 10.0**(-p)
    d2 = (math.sin(1-h) - 2*math.sin(1) + math.sin(1+h))/h**2
    roundoff.append([p, h, abs(d2 + math.sin(1))])

def solve(steps, backward):
    dt = 1.0/steps
    u = 1.0
    for _ in range(steps):
        if backward:
            u /= 1 + 50*dt
        else:
            u *= 1 - 50*dt
    return u

stability = []
for n in (50, 26, 25, 24, 20):
    dt = 1.0/n
    stability.append([n, dt, 1 - 50*dt, abs(solve(n, False)), solve(n, True)])

euler = []
exact = math.exp(-50.0)
for n in (8000, 16000, 32000, 64000, 128000):
    error = abs(solve(n, False) - exact)
    order = math.log2(euler[-1][2]/error) if euler else None
    euler.append([n, 1.0/n, error, order])
assert min(roundoff, key=lambda row: row[-1])[0] == 4
assert stability[2][2:4] == [-1.0, 1.0]
assert all(euler[i][2] < euler[i-1][2] for i in range(1, len(euler)))
(work / 'numerical_data.json').write_text(json.dumps(dict(roundoff=roundoff, stability=stability, euler=euler)), encoding='utf-8')

plt.rcParams.update({'font.size': 10.8, 'axes.spines.top': False, 'axes.spines.right': False})
fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(6.6, 2.65), layout='constrained')
h = [r[1] for r in roundoff]
errors = [r[2] for r in roundoff]
ax.loglog(h, errors, 'o-', lw=1.7, color='#176078')
ax.scatter([h[3]], [errors[3]], color='#a45421', s=45, zorder=4, label='Smallest sampled error')
ax.set(xlabel='Grid spacing h', ylabel='Absolute error')
ax.set_xticks([10.0**k for k in range(-10, 0)])
ax.xaxis.set_minor_formatter(NullFormatter())
ax.grid(which='major', alpha=.19)
ax.legend(frameon=False, loc='upper right')
fig.savefig(work/'roundoff.png', dpi=220)
plt.close(fig)

fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(6.6, 2.62), layout='constrained')
dt = [r[1] for r in euler]
err = [r[2] for r in euler]
ax.loglog(dt, err, 'o-', lw=1.7, color='#176078', label='Forward Euler')
ax.loglog(dt, [err[-1] * d / dt[-1] for d in dt], '--', color='#707070', label='Slope 1')
ax.set(xlabel='Time step Δt', ylabel='Absolute error at t = 1')
ax.grid(which='major', alpha=.20)
ax.legend(frameon=False, loc='upper left')
fig.savefig(work/'euler.png', dpi=220)
plt.close(fig)
print(work)
`;

section(2, 'Taylor expansions')
task('2.1', 'Expansions at nearby points')
body('All derivatives on the right are evaluated at x. Keep terms through fourth order.')
eq('(a)  f(x+h) = f(x) + hf′ + (h²/2)f″ + (h³/6)f‴ + (h⁴/24)f^{(4)} + O(h⁵).')
eq('(b)  f(x−h) = f(x) − hf′ + (h²/2)f″ − (h³/6)f‴ + (h⁴/24)f^{(4)} + O(h⁵).')
body('The odd powers change sign because replacing h by −h multiplies the kth term by (−1)ᵏ.')
eq('(c)  f(x+2h) = f(x) + 2hf′ + 2h²f″ + (4h³/3)f‴ + (2h⁴/3)f^{(4)} + O(h⁵).')

section(3, 'Finite differences', page=False)
task('3.1', 'Backward difference')
body('Substitute the expansion of f(x − h) into the numerator and divide by h.')
eq('D₋f(x) = [f(x) − f(x−h)]/h = f′(x) − (h/2)f″(x) + O(h²).')
body('The leading error D₋f − f′ is −(h/2)f″. The backward difference is first-order accurate.')

task('3.2', 'Central second difference')
body('(a–b) Add the expansions at x + h and x − h. The odd terms cancel. After subtracting 2f(x) and dividing by h², the result is')
eq('D₂f(x) = [f(x−h) − 2f(x) + f(x+h)]/h².')
eq('D₂f(x) − f″(x) = (h²/12) f^{(4)}(x) + O(h⁴).')
body('The leading error is proportional to h², so the approximation is second-order accurate.')
body('(c) For f(x) = sin(x) at x = 1, the exact second derivative is −sin(1).')
hvals = (0.1, 0.05, 0.025, 0.0125)
err = [abs((math.sin(1-h) - 2*math.sin(1) + math.sin(1+h))/h**2 + math.sin(1)) for h in hvals]
table(('h','Absolute error','Previous error ÷ current error'),[
    (f'{h:g}',f'{e:.9f}',f'{err[i-1]/e:.3f}' if i else '—')
    for i,(h,e) in enumerate(zip(hvals,err))
], (1,1.65,3.04))
body('Halving h reduces the error by about a factor of four, as expected.')

task('3.3', 'Checks on the formula')
body('(a) A constant gives numerator 1 − 2 + 1 = 0. A linear function gives (x−h) − 2x + (x+h) = 0. For x², the numerator is (x−h)² − 2x² + (x+h)² =<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="480" /> in the numerator loses precision, leaving an error of order ε that is divided by h². Balancing h² and ε/h² gives h of order ε¹ᐟ⁴, about 10⁻⁴ in double precision. Below this, cancellation dominates and the individual errors fluctuate.')

section(4, 'Time discretization')
task('4.1', 'Forward and backward Euler')
body('(a) For u′ = −50u with u(0) = 1, the exact value at t = 1 is exp(−50). We used K steps with Δt = 1/K and iterated both updates in Python.')
code('def euler(steps, backward=False):\n'
     '    dt = 1.0 / steps\n'

     '    for _ in range(steps):\n'
     '        u = u / (1 + 50*dt) if backward else (1 - 50*dt)*u\n'

body('(b) Forward Euler multiplies u by 1 − 50Δt. Its magnitude is bounded if that multiplier lies in [−1, 1]. The largest stable step is 0.04. At equality the values alternate between ±1. At K = 24 and K = 20 the magnitudes grow.')

],(.82,1.00,1.68,1.37,1.59),'Stability runs with both updates.')

table(('Steps K','Δt','Absolute error at t = 1','Observed order'),[
    (f'{n:,}',sci(dt,3),sci(e),f'{order:.4f}' if order else '—') for n,dt,e,order in data['euler']

section(5, 'Discretizing the heat equation')
task('5.1', 'One dimensional update')
body('(a) For an interior node, use the second difference in the method of lines. The prescribed Dirichlet values supply the end nodes.')
eq('du_{i}/dt = α[u_{i−1} − 2u_{i} + u_{i+1}]/Δx².')
body('(b–c) Forward Euler evaluates that right-hand side at time level n. The dimensionless parameter is r = αΔt/Δx².')
eq('u^{n+1}_{i} = (1 − 2r)u^{n}_{i} + r(u^{n}_{i−1} + u^{n}_{i+1}).')
body('The combination is the same as αt/L² in Task 1.2, now using one time step and one grid spacing.')

task('5.2', 'Two dimensional update')
body('(a) The update at an interior node adds αΔt times the two spatial second differences from Task 3.4, each evaluated at time level n.')
eq('u^{n+1}(i,j) = u^{n}(i,j) + αΔt[D_{xx}u^{n}(i,j) + D_{yy}u^{n}(i,j)].')
body('(b) For equal spacing h, set r = αΔt/h². The center weight is 1 − 4r and each neighbor has weight r.')
eq('u^{n+1}(i,j) = (1 − 4r)u^{n}(i,j) + r[u^{n}(i−1,j) + u^{n}(i+1,j) + u^{n}(i,j−1) + u^{n}(i,j+1)].')
body('(c) The four neighbors and the center come from the old grid. Only the new center value is written.')
code('Old level n                         New level n + 1\n'
     '          Top\n'
     'Left     Center     Right    ────>        Center\n'
     '         Bottom')
body('(d) With the weights precomputed, summing the four neighbors takes three additions. Two multiplications and one final addition give six floating-point operations per update. Each update uses five input grid values and writes one. Cache reuse reduces the number of memory accesses that reach RAM.')

task('5.3', 'Interpreting the weights')
body('(a) The weights sum to (1 − 4r) + 4r = 1. If the five input values all equal C, the new value remains C. A uniform plate stays uniform when the boundaries are compatible.')
body('(b) The change equals 4r times the four-neighbor average minus the old cent<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1007" />en needed to reach T exactly.')
body('(b–c) At six operations per interior update, the work is 6K(N−1)² ≈ 2.4N⁴ operations. Doubling N therefore increases the work and the estimated runtime by about 16.')
table(('N','Steps K','Floating-point operations','Time at 10⁹ operations/s'),[
    (f'{n:,}', f'{(2*n*n+4)//5:,}', sci(6*((2*n*n+4)//5)*(n-1)**2), time)
    for n,time in ((1000,'2,395 s (39.9 min)'),(4000,'614,093 s (7.11 days)'))
],(.68,.98,2.16,2.60), '(d) Estimates based on the six-operation stencil count.')

body('The error was small at 300 steps. At 400 steps the oscillations are most visible in the plate interior near the center. By 500 steps the error exceeds 1. The snapshots locate the early visible pattern without identifying an exact onset step.')

section(7, 'Verifying the solver')
task('7.1', 'Expected convergence order')
body('Task 5.5 gives a time term of order Δt and a space term of order h². With r fixed, Δt = rh²/α, so both terms scale as h². For this stable, smooth case we expect order two in h. Halving h should reduce the error by about four.')

task('7.2', 'Case V convergence study')
body('(a) The serial solver used Case V with α = 1, T = 0.05, and r = 0.2 at N = 16, 32, 64, 128, and 256. The step count T/(rh²) was an integer on each grid.')
body('(b) At the final time, compare the numerical field with sin(πx) sin(πy) exp(−2π²T). We measure the maximum nodal error and the discrete L₂ error, which is h times the square root of the sum of squared nodal errors.')

    for row in convergence
],(.54,.62,.81,1.55,1.63,1.35),'Saved serial results at T = 0.05.')

`--out` writes the full `(N+1) × (N+1)` field, including boundaries, in row-major order with `i` varying fastest. Values are native binary doubles, which are little-endian on the project's x86-64 Ubuntu VMs. The file has no header.

## Convergence study

Task 7.2 uses Case V, `r=0.2`, and `T=0.05` on five grids:

```bash
for N in 16 32 64 128 256; do
    ./heat --decomp serial --case V --N "$N" --r 0.2 --T 0.05
done
python3 scripts/plot_convergence.py
```

The plotting script reads the saved [solver output](results/convergence_runs.txt) and regenerates the [convergence table](results/convergence.csv) and [log-log plot](results/convergence.png). The measured discrete L2 error drops by about a factor of four on each refinement. The observed order approaches 2.

## Deliberate instability test

Task 6.7 uses the separate experimental executable at `r=0.2625`, 5% above the production solver's stability bound:

```bash
make unstable_demo
./unstable_demo --case V --N 16 --r 0.2625 --steps 1000 --out results/instability_1000.bin
python3 scripts/plot_instability.py
```

The saved [1,000-step output](results/instability_1000.txt) and [checkerboard image](results/instability.png) show growing grid-scale oscillations. Earlier logs in `results/` record how the error develops.

CXX = g++
CXXFLAGS = -std=c++17 -O2 -ffp-contract=off

all: heat

heat: src/serial.cpp
	$(CXX) $(CXXFLAGS) src/serial.cpp -o heat

unstable_demo: src/serial_unstable_demo.cpp
	$(CXX) $(CXXFLAGS) $< -o $@

# Convenient alias for building the serial implementation.
serial: heat

clean:
	rm -f heat unstable_demo

.PHONY: all serial clean

BODY Normal Part 1: Serial heat solver and verification
BODY Normal We implemented and verified a serial solver for heat diffusion on a unit square. The Case V errors decreased by a factor of about four each time the grid spacing was halved. A separate test above the stability limit produced a growing checkerboard pattern.
BODY Heading 1 Numerical method
BODY Normal We use N intervals along each axis, so the spacing is h = 1/N and the grid contains (N + 1)² nodes. Thermal diffusivity is α = 1. At each step, the explicit five-point stencil updates an interior node from its old value and the four adjacent values.
BODY Normal 
BODY Normal 
BODY Normal Here r = Δt/h². The stable solver accepts 0 < r ≤ 0.25. For a requested final time T, it rounds the step count up and divides T by that count so the run reaches T within the requested time-step limit.
BODY Heading 1 Cases and implementation
BODY Normal Cases 1 and 2 start with a cold interior. Case 1 holds all edges at 1. Case 2 holds the top and bottom edges, including their corners, at 1 and the remaining side nodes at 0. Case V starts from sin(πx) sin(πy) with zero edge values.
BODY Normal The C++17 solver stores each time level in a contiguous row-major array. It holds the edges fixed, updates the interior into the second array, and swaps the arrays after each step. The Makefile uses -O2 and -ffp-contract=off. We time only the step loop with std::chrono::steady_clock. The optional binary output stores the full grid as headerless little-endian doubles in row-major order.
BODY Heading 1 Verification
BODY Normal For Cases 1 and 2, the saved checks at N = 8, r = 0.2, and 20 steps confirmed the prescribed edges and temperatures between 0 and 1.
BODY Normal For Case V, the exact solution is sin(πx) sin(πy) exp(−2π²t). At T = 0.05 with r = 0.2, the observed order of the discrete L₂ error approached 2 across N = 16, 32, 64, 128, a<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="346" />bug\ndone\n"$part1/heat" --case V --N 16 --r 0.2625 --steps 1000'

Part 2: MPI 1D strip decomposition
Using MPI_Wtime, the program measures the stepping loop, computation and halo communication. MPI_Reduce with MPI_MAX reports the maximum time across ranks for each measurement. Setup, output and error checking are excluded from the timed loop.
We checked Cases 1, 2 and V using N = 8, 10 and 16, P = 1, 2, 3 and 4, r = 0.2, and 10 steps. All 36 local MPI runs matched the serial output bit for bit using cmp. The professor’s halo-debug tests also passed for N = 8, Case 2, one step and P = 2 or 3, including the uneven division. The MPI solver was then tested across two VMs and three VMs. Both distributed Case 2 outputs matched the serial result bit for bit (N = 8, 10 steps).
For small grids, communication dominates computation. In the three-VM Case 2 run, t_total was 1.2462 s while the maximum measured computation time was about 0.000179 s. These are individual measurements, not reliable speedup benchmarks. Blocking MPI_Send calls can deadlock if ranks wait for matching receives, especially with large messages. MPI_Sendrecv avoids this unsafe send-then-receive pattern.
An additional three-VM Case V run was attempted but could not be completed reliably because the MPI runtime lost communication with remote daemons. CXX = mpic++
CXXFLAGS = -std=c++17 -O2 -ffp-contract=off

all: heat_strips

heat_strips: src/strips.cpp
	$(CXX) $(CXXFLAGS) $< -o $@

clean:
	rm -f heat_strips

.PHONY: all clean
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

mkdir -p results/correctness

passed=0

for N in 8 10 16; do
  for CASE in 1 2 V; do

    SERIAL="results/correctness/serial_${CASE}_${N}.bin"

    ../part1/heat --decomp serial \
      --case "$CASE" --N "$N" \
      --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
      --out "$SERIAL" > /dev/null

    for P in 1 2 3 4; do

      MPI="results/correctness/mpi_${CASE}_${N}_${P}.bin"

      mpirun --oversubscribe -np "$P" ./heat_strips \
        --case "$CASE" --N "$N" \
        --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
        --out "$MPI" > /dev/null

      if cmp -s "$SERIAL" "$MPI"; then
        echo "PASS: Case=$CASE N=$N P=$P"
        passed=$((passed + 1))
      else
        echo "FAIL: Case=$CASE N=$N P=$P"
        exit 1
      fi

    done
  done
done

echo "--------------------------------"

## Distributed Execution over Tailscale

The project uses three Ubuntu VMs:

| VM | Tailscale IP |
|---|---|
| heat-1 | 100.100.125.10 |
| heat-2 | 100.98.24.109 |
| heat-3 | 100.113.165.26 |

All participating VMs must be powered on, connected to Tailscale and have the MPI executable available at the same path.

The solver is compiled on each VM in:

```text
/home/mpi/part2/
```

### Three-VM Hostfile

Create a file named `hosts_3vm`:

```text
100.98.24.109 slots=1
100.100.125.10 slots=1
100.113.165.26 slots=1
```

### Verify Connectivity

```bash
mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm -np 3 \
  --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl tcp,self \
  hostname
```

The output must contain all three VM hostnames.

### Distributed Case 2

```bash
mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm -np 3 \
  --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl tcp,self \
  ./heat_strips \
  --decomp strips --case 2 \
  --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
  --out results/distributed_case2_N8_P3.bin
```

Generate the serial reference:

```bash
../part1/heat --decomp serial \
  --case 2 --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
  --out results/serial_case2_N8_steps10.bin
```

Compare:

```bash
cmp results/serial_case2_N8_steps10.bin \
    results/distributed_case2_N8_P3.bin
```

Case 2 was successfully verified across both two and three VMs.

## Distributed Case V Verification

On November 24, 2026, Case V was successfully executed across all three Ubuntu VMs.

Parameters:

- N=16
- P=3
- r=0.2
- Steps=10
- T=0.0078125

### MPI Execution

```bash
timeout 60s mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm -np 3 \
  --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl tcp,self \
  ./heat_strips \
  --decomp strips --case V \
  --N 16 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
  --out results/distributed_caseV_short.bin
```

### Serial Reference

```bash
../part1/heat --decomp serial \
  --case V --N 16 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
  --out results/serial_caseV_short.bin
```

### Correctness Verification

```bash
cmp results/serial_caseV_short.bin \
    results/distributed_caseV_short.bin
```

SHA-256 verification:

```bash
sha256sum results/serial_caseV_short.bin \
          results/distributed_caseV_short.bin
```

Both binary files had identical SHA-256 hashes.

Final result:

```text
PASS: Three-VM Case V, N=16, P=3, r=0.2, steps=10, bit-for-bit identical
```

### Case V Results

| Measurement | Serial | MPI - 3 VMs |
|---|---:|---:|
| t_total (s) | 0.000008669 | 0.779550588 |
| t_comp (s) | 0.000008669 | 0.000008195 | 
| t_comm (s) | 0 | 0.779543372 |
| err_inf | 0.000598805794 | 0.000598805794 |
| err_l2h | 0.000299402897 | 0.000299402897 |

Both implementations produced identical temperature fields and numerical errors.

]:

    style.font.bold = bold
    style.font.italic = False
    style.font.color.rgb = RGBColor(0,0,0)
    fmt = style.paragraph_format
    fmt.space_before, fmt.space_after = Pt(before), Pt(after)
    fmt.line_spacing = 1.10 if name=='Normal' else 1.02
    fmt.widow_control = True
    if name == 'Title' or name.startswith('Heading'):
        fmt.keep_with_next = True
        fmt.keep_together = True
    props = style.element.get_or_add_pPr()
    for border in props.findall(qn('w:pBdr')):
        props.remove(border)

steps = []

The only change is the prescribed boundary temperature.')\ncode('./heat --decomp serial --case 1 --N 64 --r 0.2 --steps 100 \\\\\\n'\n     '    --out \"$demo_dir/case1.bin\"\\n'\n     './heat --decomp serial --case 2 --N 64 --r 0.2 --steps 100 \\\\\\n'\n     '    --out \"$demo_dir/case2.bin\"', bash=True)\nnote('Expected.', 'Both summaries report P=1, N=64, r=0.2, steps=100, T=0.0048828125, and t_comm=0. Each run has equal t_total and t_comp. Only Case V reports error norms.')\nbody(f'Both binary fields contain 65 × 65 double values and should be {binary_size:,} bytes each.')\ncode('wc -c \"$demo_dir/case1.bin\" \"$demo_dir/case2.bin\"', bash=True)\np = doc.add_paragraph()\np.alignment = WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.CENTER\np.paragraph_format.keep_with_next = True\np.paragraph_format.space_after = Pt(1)\nfor i in (1,2):\n    if i==2:\n        p.add_run('  ')\n    img = p.add_run().add_picture(str(results/f'demo_case{i}.png'), width=Inches(3.18))\n    img._inline.docPr.set('descr',f'Saved serial heat map for Case {i} after 100 time steps.')\ndoc.add_paragraph('Saved images for Case 1 and Case 2 after 100 steps. Both use the same temperature scale from 0 to 1.', 'Caption')\nnote('Case 1.', 'All four edges are held at 1, so the heat map has a bright border on every side.')\nnote('Case 2.', 'The top and bottom edges, including the corners, are held at 1. The remaining side nodes are held at 0. The heat map has two bright horizontal bands.')\nnote('What to say.', '“The same stencil and time step give different patterns because the edges supply heat differently. The center stays cooler than the hot edges in these short runs.”')\n\nheading('Optional stability check',level=2)\ncode('./heat --decomp serial --case V --N 16 --r 0.2625 --steps 1', bash=True)\nnote('Expected.', 'The solver exits with an error and prints “Invalid parameters. Use N >= 2, steps >= 0, and 0 < r <= 0.25.” The requested r is 5% above the stability limit.')\n\nheading('Regenerate the heat maps',page=True)\nbody('Optional. The saved heat maps on the previous page can be shown directly. To plot the new binary fields, run this block on the same Ubuntu VM after Step 3. Python 3 and Matplotlib are required.')\nplot = '''python3 - \"$demo_dir\" <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport struct\nimport sys\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use(\"Agg\")\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\nfolder = Path(sys.argv[1])\nwidth = 65\nfor case in (1, 2):\n    source = folder / f\"case{case}.bin\"\n    data = struct.unpack(f\"<{width*width}d\", source.read_bytes())\n    grid = [data[j*width:(j+1)*width] for j in range(width)]\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(6, 5))\n    im = ax.imshow(grid, origin=\"lower\", extent=[0, 1, 0, 1],\n                   cmap=\"inferno\", vmin=0, vmax=1)\n    ax.set(xlabel=\"x\", ylabel=\"y\", title=f\"Case {case} after 100 steps\")\n    fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax, label=\"Temperature\")\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    output = folder / f\"case{case}.png\"\n    fig.savefig(output, dpi=150)\n    plt.close(fig)\n    print(f\"Saved {output}\")\nPY'''\ncompile(plot.partition('\\n')[2].rsplit('\\n',1)[0],'<plot demo>','exec')\ncode(plot,bash=True)\nnote('Expected.', 'The script prints two Saved lines. import os
import re
from pathlib import Path
from io import BytesIO
from zipfile import ZipFile
from docx import Document
from docx.enum.table import WD_TABLE_ALIGNMENT, WD_CELL_VERTICAL_ALIGNMENT
from docx.enum.text import WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH
from docx.oxml import OxmlElement
from docx.oxml.ns import qn
from docx.shared import Inches, Pt, RGBColor

qa = Path(os.environ['PART2_REPORT_QA'])
repo = Path.cwd()
desktop = Path('C:/Users/User/Desktop')
original = desktop/'Part2_Report_Simple_Copy_Paste.docx'
with ZipFile(original) as z:
    visuals = {i:z.read(f'word/media/image{i}.png') for i in (1,2,3,4)}
logs = repo/'part2/results'
local = (logs/'step5_correctness_log.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
assert len(re.findall(r'^PASS: Case=', local, re.M)) == 36
dist = (logs/'distributed_caseV_verification.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
hashes = re.findall(r'^[a-f0-9]{64}',dist,re.M)
assert len(hashes) == 2 and hashes[0] == hashes[1]
assert 'N=16, P=3, r=0.2, steps=10' in dist
source = Document(original)
running = source.tables[-1]
case2_rows = [
    tuple(c.text for c in row.cells[1:])
    for row in running.rows[1:]
]
assert len(case2_rows) == 2

doc = Document()
doc.core_properties.title = 'Part 2 MPI Strip Decomposition'
doc.core_properties.author = ''
sec = doc.sections[0]
